Alex Paige Moore 8 · Critical ↓ declining

Down 38 from 46 at w/e 2026-09-11, the fifth consecutive decline (58 → 64 → 41 → 52 → 48 unpublished → 46 → 8). The client terminated the relationship on 2026-09-17 and ALTR did not respond to it inside the window. That alone caps this score in single digits; everything else is footnote. The mechanics that remained were billing mechanics: 10 of ALTR's 13 messages this week were invoicing, including a chase that called her weekly-instalment arrangement a 'missed payment' one working day after she explained it for the third time, a request to pull an OF withdrawal early that ignores the two-week payout lag she documented in 2025, and a fifth banking change in 12 months delivered inside an invoice message. Credit where it is genuinely due: Stephski finally put a real number in the chat ($7,582.74, the first figure since Aug 13) and made the first-ever attempt to move her onto a weekly cadence ('Maybe you can send every thursday if that works for you?'); Mica resumed real reel inspo after 33 days and sent a proactive Instagram security advisory. But the surgery disclosed on Aug 26 and repeated on Sep 2 was never acknowledged by anyone across 23 days and four report windows; Derrek's silence reached 36 days and did not break even when she quit; Michelle Queue's eight-week leak-report streak broke in the churn week; @paigealexxo stayed dark at 108 days from her first ask; the trial-reels call died unchased at 14 days; and every item on the stale-promise stack aged another seven days untouched. She left warmly, thanking a team that spent her final week asking her for money, a Facebook account and more reels. That is not a pricing loss or a performance loss — it is a relationship loss, and the report has named it in five consecutive windows.

THE CLIENT RESIGNED. On 2026-09-17 at 20:19 Alex Paige wrote: 'Hey guys I just want to let you know I’m leaving ALTR. I want to thankyou all so much for helping me with everything you’ve been amazing. I wish you all the best with your futures!' Thirty-one minutes later Stephski posted in the chat — 'okay perfect thank you :)' — answering her bank-account question in the Invoicing topic and saying nothing whatsoever about the resignation. When the window closed 12 hours and 46 minutes later, not one person at ALTR had acknowledged that the client had left. No question, no call request, no save attempt, no exit conversation. Derrek has still not written in this chat since 2026-08-13 — 36 days — and did not write when she quit. This was the account's fifth consecutive declining week and the exit was fully signposted: she has quietly left a management before ('I did tell the owner I was leaving and gave notice', 2025-09-24) and last week's report flagged the near-silence of Sep 5-11 as the same pattern that preceded the surgery blackout. What she actually received in her final seven days was 18 messages, 13 of them from ALTR, and 10 of those 13 were billing. Stephski chased on Sep 13 ('checking in on next payment since this was on the 3rd 🙏'), proposed a weekly Thursday cadence, then on Sep 14 told her 'We are currently still at $7,582.74 owed from last invoice due to missed payment last week' and asked her to 'initiate a OF withdrawl today' — a request that directly contradicts the payout timing she had explained for the third time three days earlier ('I just pay 30% each pay I recieve each week from Of', Sep 11). On Sep 15 the Sep 1-14 invoice arrived carrying ALTR's FIFTH banking change in 12 months ('Please note a slight name change to the account'), which immediately produced 'Can you confirm the account you sent to?' on Sep 16. The only non-billing ALTR contact in her last week was two Mica broadcasts (reel links Sep 15, an Instagram security notice Sep 17) and one Mayson extraction ask — 'Hey @alexpaigem do you have a facebook account available to use?' (Sep 16), a brand-new platform demand to a client 21 days post-surgery, never answered. In 23 days since 'Hey I’ve just had surgery so probably not for a few weeks unfortunately' not a single person at ALTR ever asked how she was. She paid anyway: on Sep 16 she posted a receipt and 'Sent last 2 weeks', honouring the promise she made on Sep 14, while already deciding to leave. Her amount, again, appears nowhere — the fifth consecutive payment with no figure recorded. She exits with an unquantified balance on the Aug 15-31 invoice, a Sep 1-14 invoice issued two days before she quit, a $1,989 dispute open 53 days, and ALTR still owing her an unstated referral balance 36 days past 'Sending'. Michelle Queue's leak-removal report — the only unbroken cadence on the account for eight weeks — did not arrive on Sep 15 either. @paigealexxo never posted. The trial-reels call was never chased. Nothing on the stale-promise stack moved.

The aggravating detail is that our own Social Media Director posted a formal advisory in this chat at 11:03 on 09-17 saying 'Please do not send your backup codes to anyone, including our team', and Mayson asked her for one at 14:51 the same day.
